Hi, Doctor. Thank you for fitting me in. I, uh... I hope I'm in the right place for this. It’s a little embarrassing to admit, to be honest. I don't want to make a big fuss, but... well, I've been having these times where I just eat way too much.

Well, I... I know we've kind of circled around this, and I really don't want to sound like I'm just completely falling apart or anything. I mean, out on the road, I'm disciplined. I have to be. I manage my routes, my time, everything.

Well, um... I know you just asked about my mood a minute ago, and I don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm not listening to you. I really am trying to give you the best answers I can.

Well, um... I know we touched on this just a second ago, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much or wasting your time. I know you have a lot of patients to see.

Since I'm driving the truck most of the day, I can't exactly go to a gym and run it off, either. I just kind of live with the guilt, I guess, and try to promise myself I'll be better the next day. \n\nIs... is that what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating during these episodes where you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try to be in control. I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just completely reckless or anything. I'm usually a very disciplined guy. But... to be completely honest with you, once I start eating during those times, it's like... it feels like that same discipline I have out on the road just sort of shuts off. \n\nI'll actually be sitting there in the cab or at home, literally telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down. You've had enough.' But... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. It's really hard to admit that to you, a grown man not being able to stop his own hand. I hope I'm explaining that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's happening.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating too much happen?\n\nPatient: Well, uh... I guess it happens more than I'd like to admit. I don't want to make it sound worse than it is, but... since I've been stuck at home with this back injury, it's gotten a bit more frequent. \n\nI'd say it happens, you know, several nights most weeks. I try really hard to have good weeks, but... if I'm being completely honest, I can't really think of a single week in the last three months where I didn't have at least one of these... episodes. \n\nI know that probably sounds like a lot, and I'm really trying to get a handle on it. I hope that's the kind of detail you need? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate timeline so you can help me.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating too much usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset about something?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I guess I wouldn't really say it's just when I'm upset. I mean, I try to handle stress pretty well, you know? Being a driver, you kind of have to roll with the punches. \n\nSo, it's not like I'm having a breakdown every time I eat too much. It just sort of... happens. Sometimes I'm just sitting there watching TV, not really feeling anxious at all, and I still end up eating more than I should. I don't want you to think I'm an overly emotional guy who can't handle his problems. \n\nIs that helpful? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way. I know some people eat their feelings, but I don't think that's exactly what's going on with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I think we kind of touched on that a little bit earlier, didn't we? I mean, I really don't want to sound like a broken record, or like I'm just completely out of control. \n\nBut, um... yeah, like I was saying before, once I get going, it's... it's incredibly hard to just put the brakes on. I try, I really do try to stop myself. But it's like my hand has a mind of its own. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so we can figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or self-critical after these episodes of eating too much?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess this is the hardest part to admit. I really don't want you to think less of me, you know? I've always prided myself on being a steady, dependable guy... the kind of man who has things under control. \n\nBut... yeah. Honestly, that's a big part of why I'm here. After it happens, I just... I sit there looking at all the empty wrappers and containers, and I just feel so... disgusted with myself. It's a terrible feeling, like I've completely let myself down. I don't want to sound dramatic, but it really eats at me. \n\nI hope it's okay that I'm being this honest with you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the whole picture, even the parts I'm not proud of.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood or emotional state plays a role in when these episodes of eating too much happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know we talked a little bit about stress earlier, and I... I really don't want to frustrate you by saying the same thing again. But honestly, I don't really think my mood has much to do with it. \n\nI've always been a pretty even-keeled guy, you know? I don't really get too high or too low. So, it's not like I'm feeling sad or angry or anything when I start eating. Sometimes I'm in a perfectly good mood, just resting up after a long shift on the road, and it still happens. I just haven't really noticed a pattern with my emotions. \n\nI'm... I'm not sure if that's the answer you're looking for? I'm sorry if I'm not giving you a very clear reason for why this is happening. I can definitely try to pay more attention to my moods if you think that's something I should be doing? I just want to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I know we've kind of circled around this, and I really don't want to sound like I'm just completely falling apart or anything. I mean, out on the road, I'm disciplined. I have to be. I manage my routes, my time, everything. \n\nBut when I'm eating like that... it's like all that discipline just shuts off. I'll actually be sitting there, telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down. You don't need this.' But... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. It's embarrassing to admit that to you, honestly. A man my age should have better control. \n\nI hope I'm making sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you can understand what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating in response to an emotional need during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know you just asked about my mood a minute ago, and I don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm not listening to you. I really am trying to give you the best answers I can. \n\nBut... no, I really don't think it's an emotional thing. Like I said, I'm a pretty steady guy. I don't get all worked up about things. I know sometimes people eat when they're upset or lonely, but... that just doesn't feel like what's happening with me. It's more like... I just start eating, and then I can't stop, regardless of how I'm feeling inside. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't have a better explanation for you? I know doctors usually like to find a root cause for these things, and I feel bad that I can't point to an emotion and say, 'That's it.' Am I doing okay with these questions? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control over your eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know we touched on this just a second ago, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much or wasting your time. I know you have a lot of patients to see. \n\nBut yes, I guess... I guess \"losing control\" is probably the right way to put it. It's just so strange for me, because out on the road, I'm the guy who has everything under control, you know? I have to be. But once I start eating during these... times... it's like all that discipline just completely shuts off. \n\nI'll literally be sitting there, fully aware, telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down. You don't need this.' But... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. It's like I'm watching myself do it and I can't stop it. It's really hard to admit that out loud, because I know I should be stronger than that. \n\nI hope that answers your question better? I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so we can figure this out. I'm trying my best to be clear for you.\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating too much been happening for more than 3 months?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I hope I'm getting the timeline right for you, I don't want to give you bad information. But, um, yeah, I'd say it's been right around that three-month mark. \n\nIt all really started when I strained my back and they had to take me off the road. Being stuck at home instead of driving my routes... I guess that's when this whole eating thing kind of snuck up on me. It's been about three months since the injury, so that lines up. \n\nI really hope that's the kind of detail you're looking for? I know doctors need accurate timelines, and I'm trying to be as precise as I can so I'm not making your job harder. Does that three-month timeframe make sense with what you usually see?\n\nDoctor: Do you consider these episodes of eating too much to be times when you're eating an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat in a similar time frame?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really want to be honest with you here, even though it's a little embarrassing to admit. I hope you don't judge me too harshly for this, I really am trying to be a good patient for you. \n\nBut... yeah, I suppose it is a lot more than a normal person would eat. Like, some evenings, I'll tell myself I'm just going to heat up what I swear is just one bowl of pozole, you know? Just a regular portion. But then... before I even really realize what's happening, I've gone through the entire pot. And then maybe a couple of tamales, and chips, and... well, whatever leftovers are sitting there in the kitchen. It's honestly like I'm feeding a whole crew instead of just myself. \n\nI know that's probably not what you want to hear, and I feel awful even saying it out loud to you. I should be managing things better. Am I giving you the right kind of details, though? I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you need to know so I'm not making your job any harder.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I hope this doesn't make me sound completely hopeless. I really am trying to be disciplined, just like I always was when I was driving. I want you to know I'm not usually like this. 

But... yeah, it's like... once I start, it feels like that discipline just completely shuts off. It's the strangest thing. Even while I'm sitting there, literally telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down, you've had enough,' my hand just... it just keeps going back for more. It's like I can't stop it, even though my brain knows I should. 

I know that sounds terrible, and I feel so weak even admitting it to you. I should have better self-control at my age. I'm really trying, I promise. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it clearly enough for you.

So, it's not like I'm feeling sad or angry or anything when I start eating. Sometimes I'm in a perfectly good mood, just resting up after a long shift on the road, and it still happens. I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you can understand what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating in response to an emotional need during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really am trying to give you the best answers I can. \n\nBut... no, I really don't think it's an emotional thing. Like I said, I'm a pretty steady guy. I don't get all worked up about things. I know sometimes people eat when they're upset or lonely, but... that just doesn't feel like what's happening with me. It's more like... I just start eating, and then I can't stop, regardless of how I'm feeling inside. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't have a better explanation for you? I know doctors usually like to find a root cause for these things, and I feel bad that I can't point to an emotion and say, 'That's it.' Am I doing okay with these questions? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control over your eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess \"losing control\" is probably the right way to put it. It's just so strange for me, because out on the road, I'm the guy who has everything under control, you know? I have to be. You don't need this.' But... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. It's like I'm watching myself do it and I can't stop it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really hope this doesn't sound like I'm just making excuses. I've always tried to be the kind of guy who just deals with things, you know? Not someone who lets his feelings push him around. 

But... honestly? I'm not really sure if it's tied to my emotions. I haven't really noticed a pattern like that. It's more just... being stuck at home with this back thing, not out on the road where I belong. It's just... frustrating, I guess? But I wouldn't say I'm eating because I'm sad or angry or anything like that. At least, I don't think I am. 

I really hope that's an okay answer? I just haven't really put two and two together in that way, but if you think I should be paying closer attention to my feelings before it happens, I can definitely start doing that. I want to make sure I'm doing everything right to help you figure this out.

I'm sorry if I'm not giving you a very clear reason for why this is happening. I can definitely try to pay more attention to my moods if you think that's something I should be doing? I just want to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I know we've kind of circled around this, and I really don't want to sound like I'm just completely falling apart or anything. I mean, out on the road, I'm disciplined. I have to be. I manage my routes, my time, everything. \n\nBut when I'm eating like that... it's like all that discipline just shuts off. I'll actually be sitting there, telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down. You don't need this.' But... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. It's embarrassing to admit that to you, honestly. A man my age should have better control. \n\nI hope I'm making sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you can understand what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating in response to an emotional need during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know you just asked about my mood a minute ago, and I don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm not listening to you. I really am trying to give you the best answers I can. \n\nBut... no, I really don't think it's an emotional thing. Like I said, I'm a pretty steady guy. I don't get all worked up about things. I know sometimes people eat when they're upset or lonely, but... that just doesn't feel like what's happening with me. It's more like... I just start eating, and then I can't stop, regardless of how I'm feeling inside. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't have a better explanation for you? I know doctors usually like to find a root cause for these things, and I feel bad that I can't point to an emotion and say, 'That's it.' Am I doing okay with these questions? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control over your eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know we touched on this just a second ago, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much or wasting your time. I know you have a lot of patients to see. \n\nBut yes, I guess... I guess \"losing control\" is probably the right way to put it. It's just so strange for me, because out on the road, I'm the guy who has everything under control, you know? I have to be. I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so we can figure this out. I'm supposed to be the guy setting an example, and instead I'm just hiding out at home. \n\nI feel terrible about letting them down, and I know I should be doing better. I mean, I really don't want to sound like a broken record, or like I'm just completely out of control. \n\nBut, um... yeah, like I was saying before, once I get going, it's... it's incredibly hard to just put the brakes on. I try, I really do try to stop myself. But it's like my hand has a mind of its own. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so we can figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or self-critical after these episodes of eating too much?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess this is the hardest part to admit. I really don't want you to think less of me, you know? I sit there looking at all the empty wrappers and containers, and I just feel so... disgusted with myself. It's a terrible feeling, like I've completely let myself down. I don't want to sound dramatic, but it really eats at me. \n\nI hope it's okay that I'm being this honest with you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the whole picture, even the parts I'm not proud of.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood or emotional state plays a role in when these episodes of eating too much happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so we can figure this out.
